     Enrolled Copy                                                                                S.B. 38
 1                                   Consumer Protection Modifications
                                            2026 GENERAL SESSION
                                                STATE OF UTAH
                                           Chief Sponsor: Todd Weiler
                                          House Sponsor: Ashlee Matthews
 2
 3    LONG TITLE
 4    General Description:
 5        This bill amends provisions relating to consumer protection.
 6    Highlighted Provisions:
 7        This bill:
 8        ▸ defines terms;
 9        ▸ renumbers Title 13, Chapter 2, Division of Consumer Protection;
10        ▸ renumbers Title 13, Chapter 21, Credit Services Organizations Act;
11        ▸ renumbers Title 13, Chapter 22, Charitable Solicitations Act;
12        ▸ renumbers and renames Title 13, Chapter 23, Health Spa Services Protection Act;
13        ▸ renumbers Title 13, Chapter 26, Telephone Fraud Prevention Act;
14        ▸ adds Title 13, Chapter 77, Generative Artificial Intelligence - Consumer Disclosures and
15    Enforcement, to the list of chapters the Division of Consumer Protection (division)
16    enforces;
17        ▸ amends the division's investigatory authority to investigate conduct related to statutes that
18    the division administers and enforces;
19        ▸ amends filing requirements a person makes with the division;
20        ▸ amends provisions relating to an entity's surety bond and certificate of deposit;
21        ▸ provides the circumstances under which the division may recover costs from a surety
22    bond or a certificate of deposit;
23        ▸ provides that a person correct information contained in a filing the person makes with the
24    division if the information becomes incorrect or incomplete;
25        ▸ provides that certain persons may not represent that the division or the state endorses or
26    approves the person;
27        ▸ clarifies that the issuance of certain documents does not constitute an approval or
     S.B. 38                                                                                Enrolled Copy


28     endorsement by the division or the state;
29         ▸ provides the circumstances under which the division may deny, suspend, or revoke
30     certain documents;
31         ▸ requires that a person that registers with the division maintain a registered agent in this
32     state;
33         ▸ provides requirements for the information a person shall maintain for a registered agent in
34     this state;
35         ▸ amends provisions relating to the division's enforcement powers and penalties the
36     division may impose;
37         ▸ provides the actions a court may take when the division brings an enforcement action;
38         ▸ provides the circumstances under which a court may order disgorgement of money;
39         ▸ amends charitable solicitation financial report requirements;
40         ▸ changes references in Title 13, Chapter 23, Health Spa Service Protection Act from
41     "health spa" to "fitness center";
42         ▸ provides registration and renewal requirements for certain entities and individuals;
43         ▸ provides the length of a term for certain registrations;
44         ▸ amends provisions relating to criminal background checks for an entity applying for
45     registration with the division;
46         ▸ provides that a seller or a seller's solicitor provide a consumer certain disclosures;
47         ▸ provides the circumstances under which the attorney general shall give legal advice to
48     and act as counsel for the division;
49         ▸ updates terminology to refer to the division;
50         ▸ removes redundant provisions from provisions the division enforces;
51         ▸ amends provisions relating to an annual report a person is required to file with the
52     division;
53         ▸ removes references to the attorney general's enforcement powers in relation to the
54     division; and
55         ▸ makes technical and conforming changes.
56     Money Appropriated in this Bill:
57         None
58     Other Special Clauses:
59         This bill provides a special effective date.
60     Utah Code Sections Affected:

341    Be it enacted by the Legislature of the state of Utah:
342          Section 1. Section 10-3-209 is amended to read:
343          10-3-209 (Effective 05/06/26). Personal use expenditure -- Authorized and
344    prohibited uses of campaign funds -- Enforcement -- Penalties.
345    (1) Unless a municipality adopts by ordinance more stringent definitions, the following are
346        defined terms for the purposes of this section:
347        (a) "Candidate" means a person who:
348            (i) files a declaration of candidacy for municipal office; or
349            (ii) receives contributions, makes expenditures, or gives consent for any other person
350                to receive contributions or make expenditures to bring about the person's
351                nomination or election to a public office.
352        (b) "Officeholder" means a person who is elected to and currently holds a municipal
353            office.
354        (c)(i) "Personal use expenditure" means an expenditure that:
355                (A) is not excluded from the definition of personal use expenditure by Subsection
356                      (2) and primarily furthers a personal interest of a candidate or officeholder or a
357                      candidate's or officeholder's family, which interest is not connected with the
358                      performance of an activity as a candidate or an activity or duty of an
359                      officeholder; or
360                (B) would cause the candidate or officeholder to recognize the expenditure as
361                      taxable income under federal law.
362            (ii) "Personal use expenditure" includes:
363                (A) a mortgage, rent, utility, or vehicle payment;
364                (B) a household food item or supply;
365                (C) a clothing expense, except:
366                      (I) clothing bearing the candidate's name or campaign slogan or logo that is

                                                      - 11 -
      S.B. 38                                                                                  Enrolled Copy


367                          used in the candidate's campaign;
368                      (II) clothing bearing the logo or name of a jurisdiction, district, government
369                          organization, government entity, caucus, or political party that the
370                          officeholder represents or of which the officeholder is a member; or
371                      (III) repair or replacement of clothing that is damaged while the candidate or
372                          officeholder is engaged in an activity of a candidate or officeholder;
373                 (D) an admission to a sporting, artistic, or recreational event or other form of
374                      entertainment;
375                 (E) dues, fees, or gratuities at a country club, health club, or recreational facility;
376                 (F) a salary payment made to a candidate, officeholder, or a person who has not
377                      provided a bona fide service to a candidate or officeholder;
378                 (G) a vacation;
379                 (H) a vehicle expense;
380                 (I) a meal expense;
381                 (J) a travel expense;
382                 (K) a payment of an administrative, civil, or criminal penalty;
383                 (L) a satisfaction of a personal debt;
384                 (M) a personal service, including the service of an attorney, accountant, physician,
385                      or other professional person;
386                 (N) a membership fee for a professional or service organization; and
387                 (O) a payment in excess of the fair market value of the item or service purchased.
388     (2) As used in this section, "personal use expenditure" does not mean an expenditure made:
389         (a) for a political purpose;
390         (b) for candidacy for public office;
391         (c) to fulfill a duty or activity of an officeholder;
392         (d) for a donation to a registered political party;
393         (e) for a contribution to another candidate's campaign account, including sponsorship of
394             or attendance at an event, the primary purpose of which is to solicit a contribution for
395             another candidate's campaign account;
396         (f) to return all or a portion of a contribution to a donor;
397         (g) for the following items, if made in connection with the candidacy for public office or
398             an activity or duty of an officeholder:
399             (i)(A) a mileage allowance at the rate established by the Division of Finance under
400                 Section 63A-3-107; or

                                                       - 12 -
      Enrolled Copy                                                                                     S.B. 38


401                (B) for motor fuel or special fuel, as defined in Section 59-13-102;
402            (ii) a meal expense;
403            (iii) a travel expense, including an expense incurred for airfare or a rental vehicle;
404            (iv) a payment for a service provided by an attorney or accountant;
405            (v) a tuition payment or registration fee for participation in a meeting or conference;
406            (vi) a gift;
407            (vii) a payment for the following items in connection with an office space:
408                (A) rent;
409                (B) utilities;
410                (C) a supply; or
411                (D) furnishing;
412            (viii) a booth at a meeting or event; or
413            (ix) educational material;
414        (h) to purchase or mail informational material, a survey, or a greeting card;
415        (i) for a donation to a charitable organization, as defined by Section [13-22-2] 13-22-101,
416            including admission to or sponsorship of an event, the primary purpose of which is
417            charitable solicitation, as defined in Section [13-22-2] 13-22-101;
418        (j) to repay a loan a candidate makes from the candidate's personal account to the
419            candidate's campaign account;
420        (k) to pay membership dues to a national organization whose primary purpose is to
421            address general public policy;
422        (l) for admission to or sponsorship of an event, the primary purpose of which is to
423            promote the social, educational, or economic well-being of the state or the candidate's
424            or officeholder's community;
425        (m) for one or more guests of an officeholder or candidate to attend an event, meeting,
426            or conference described in this Subsection (2); or
427        (n) to pay childcare expenses of:
428            (i) a candidate while the candidate is engaging in campaign activity; or
429            (ii) an officeholder while the officeholder is engaging in the duties of an officeholder.
430    (3)(a) A municipality may adopt an ordinance prohibiting a personal use expenditure by
431        a candidate with requirements that are more stringent than the requirements provided
432        in Subsection (4).
433        (b) The municipality may adopt definitions that are more stringent than those provided
434            in Subsection (1) or (2).

                                                     - 13 -
      S.B. 38                                                                                  Enrolled Copy


435         (c) If a municipality fails to adopt a personal use expenditure ordinance described in
436                Subsection (3)(a), a candidate shall comply with the requirements contained in
437                Subsection (4).
438     (4) A candidate or an officeholder may not use money deposited into a campaign account
439         for:
440         (a) a personal use expenditure; or
441         (b) an expenditure prohibited by law.
442     (5) A municipality may enforce this section by adopting an ordinance:
443         (a) to provide for the evaluation of a campaign finance statement to identify a personal
444                use expenditure; and
445         (b) to commence informal adjudicative proceedings if, after an evaluation described in
446                Subsection (5)(a), there is probable cause to believe that a candidate or officeholder
447                has made a personal use expenditure.
448     (6) If, in accordance with the proceedings described in Subsection (5)(b) established in
449         municipal ordinance, a municipality determines that a candidate or officeholder has
450         made a personal use expenditure, the municipality:
451         (a) may require the candidate or officeholder to:
452                (i) remit an administrative penalty of an amount equal to 50% of the personal use
453                    expenditure to the municipality; and
454                (ii) deposit the amount of the personal use expenditure into the campaign account
455                    from which the personal use expenditure was disbursed; and
456         (b) shall deposit the money received under Subsection (6)(a)(i) into the municipal
457                general fund.

644     [(3)] (4) If the division has reasonable cause to believe that a person has violated or is

945        (c) If an act or practice that violates this chapter unjustly enriches a supplier and
946            damages can be computed with reasonable certainty, damages recoverable on behalf
947            of consumers who cannot be located with due diligence shall be transferred to the
948            state treasurer in accordance with Title 67, Chapter 4a, Revised Uniform Unclaimed
949            Property Act.
950        (d) If a supplier shows by a preponderance of the evidence that a violation of this
951            chapter resulted from a bona fide error notwithstanding the maintenance of
952            procedures reasonably adapted to avoid the error, recovery under this Subsection (2)
953            is limited to the amount, if any, by which the supplier was unjustly enriched by the
954            violation.

956        action upon acceptance of the supplier's written assurance of voluntary
957        compliance with this chapter.

959                consumers or take other appropriate corrective action.

973        the supplier on whom the fine is imposed shall pay the fine in full.
974        (b) The unpaid amount of a fine is increased by 10%:
975            (i) if the fine has not been paid in full within 60 days after the final division order
976                imposing the fine; and
977            (ii) unless the division waives the 10% increase in a stipulated payment plan.
978    (6) A court shall determine the fine imposed under Subsection (1)(e) or Subsection

                                                     - 29 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


 979         (2)(b)(ii)(D) after considering the following factors:
 980         (a) the seriousness, nature, circumstances, extent, and persistence of the conduct
 981              constituting the violation, including whether the supplier acted knowingly or
 982              intentionally to deceive;
 983         (b) the harm to other persons resulting either directly or indirectly from the violation;
 984         (c) cooperation by the supplier in an inquiry or investigation conducted by the division
 985              concerning the violation;
 986         (d) efforts by the supplier to prevent occurrences of the violation;
 987         (e) efforts by the supplier to mitigate the harm caused by the violation, including a
 988              reimbursement made to a consumer injured by the act of the supplier;
 989         (f) the history of previous violations by the supplier;
 990         (g) the need to deter the supplier or other suppliers from committing the violation in the
 991              future;
 992         (h) whether the individual harmed by the violation was a vulnerable adult; and
 993         (i) other matters as justice may require.

 995             13-15-102 (Effective 05/06/26). Definitions.
 996        As used in this chapter:
 997     (1)(a) "Business opportunity" means an arrangement under which a person:
 998              (i) sells or leases a product, equipment, a supply, or a service:
 999                  (A) upon payment of initial required consideration of at least $500; and
1000                  (B) for the purpose of enabling the buyer or lessee to start a business; and
1001              (ii) represents to the buyer or lessee that:
1002                  (A) the person will provide a location or assist the buyer or lessee find a location
1003                        for the use or operation of a vending machine, rack, display case, or other
1004                        similar device, or a currency-operated amusement machine or device, on
1005                        premises neither owned nor leased by the person nor the buyer or lessee;
1006                  (B) the person will purchase a product the buyer or lessee makes, produces,
1007                        fabricates, grows, or modifies, using in whole or in part the product,
1008                        equipment, supply, or service the buyer or lessee buys or leases from the
1009                        person;
1010                  (C) the person will provide the buyer or lessee with a guarantee that the buyer or
1011                        lessee will receive income from the product, equipment, supply, or service the
1012                        buyer or lessee buys or leases from the person that exceeds the amount the

                                                         - 30 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                 S.B. 38


1013                     buyer or lessee pays to buy or lease the product, equipment, supply, or service,
1014                     and if not the person will repurchase the product, equipment, supply, or
1015                     service, if the buyer or lessee is dissatisfied; or
1016                (D) the buyer or lessee will or may derive income from the business described in
1017                     Subsection (1)(a)(i) that exceeds the amount the buyer or lessee pays to buy or
1018                     lease the product, equipment, supply, or service.
1019        (b) "Business opportunity" does not include:
1020            (i) the sale of an ongoing business when the owner of that business sells and intends
1021                to sell only that one business; or
1022            (ii) not-for-profit sale of sales demonstration equipment, materials, or samples for a
1023                total price of $500 or less.
1024    (2) "Division" means the Division of Consumer Protection [of the Department of Commerce]
1025        established by Section 13-2-102.
1026    (3) "Franchise" means the same as that term is defined by Federal Trade Commission rules
1027        governing franchise and business opportunity ventures.
1028    (4) "Guarantee" means a written agreement that:
1029        (a) a purchaser and seller sign; and
1030        (b) discloses the complete details and each limitation or exception of the agreement.
1031    (5)(a) "Initial required consideration" means the total amount a purchaser is obligated to
1032        pay under the terms of a business opportunity:
1033            (i) before the day on which the purchaser receives the product, equipment, supply, or
1034                service;
1035            (ii) the day on which the purchaser receives the product, equipment, supply, or
1036                service; or
1037            (iii) within six months after the day on which the purchaser and seller enter into the
1038                business opportunity.
1039        (b) "Initial required consideration" includes the sum of any down payment and the total
1040            of all additional payments, if the purchaser's payment under the terms of the business
1041            opportunity is over a period of time.
1042        (c) "Initial required consideration" does not include the not-for-profit sale of sales
1043            demonstration equipment, materials, or supplies for a total amount of less than $500.
1044    (6) "Principal" means as the division determines by rule made in accordance with Title
1045        63G, Chapter 3, Utah Administrative Rulemaking Act.
1046    (7) "Purchaser" means a person who buys or leases from another person a business

                                                         - 31 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                     Enrolled Copy


1047         opportunity.
1048     (8) "Registered trademark" or "service mark" means a trademark, trade name, or service
1049         mark registered with the United States Patent and Trademark Office, or Utah, or the
1050         state of incorporation if a corporation.
1051     (9)(a) "Seller" means a person who offers to sell, offers to lease, sells, or leases to
1052         another person a business opportunity or a franchise.
1053         (b) "Seller" does not include an individual representative or salesperson, unless the
1054              individual is a principal of a sole proprietorship, partnership, association, joint
1055              venture, corporation, firm, or other organization or entity used in carrying on a
1056              business, that offers to sell, offers to lease, sells, or leases to another person a
1057              business opportunity or a franchise.

1181    (2) "Credit report" means a written, oral, or other communication of information by a
1182        consumer reporting agent relating to a consumer's credit worthiness, credit standing,
                                                         - 35 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


1183         credit capacity, character, general reputation, personal characteristics, or mode of living
1184         that the consumer reporting agent uses or collects to serve as a factor in establishing the
1185         consumer's eligibility for credit for personal, family, or household purposes.
1186     [(2)] (3) "Credit reporting agency" means a person who, for a monetary fee, dues, or on a
1187         cooperative nonprofit basis, regularly engages in whole or in part in the practice of
1188         assembling or evaluating consumer credit information or other information on
1189         consumers for the purpose of furnishing consumer reports to third persons.
1190     (4)(a) "Credit score" means a numerical value or a categorization that a person:
1191             (i) derives from information in a consumer report;
1192             (ii) derives from a statistical tool or modeling system; and
1193             (iii) develops to predict the likelihood of credit behavior.
1194         (b) "Credit score" includes:
1195             (i) a risk predictor; or
1196             (ii) a risk score.
1197     [(3)] (5)(a) "Credit services organization" means a person who represents that the person
1198         or an employee is a debt professional or credit counselor, or, with respect to the
1199         extension of credit by others, sells, provides, or performs, or represents that the
1200         person can or will sell, provide, or perform, in return for the payment of money or
1201         other valuable consideration any of the following services:
1202             (i) improving a buyer's credit record, history, or rating;
1203             (ii) providing advice, assistance, instruction, or instructional materials to a buyer with
1204                 regard to Subsection [(3)(a)(i)] (5)(a)(i); or
1205             (iii) debt reduction or debt management plans.
1206         (b) "Credit services organization" does not include:
1207             (i) a person authorized to make loans or extensions of credit under the laws of this
1208                 state or the United States who is subject to regulation and supervision by this state
1209                 or the United States and who derives at least 35% of the person's income from
1210                 making loans and extensions of credit;
1211             (ii) a depository institution:
1212                 (A) as defined in Section 7-1-103; or
1213                 (B) that is regulated or supervised by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation
1214                      or the National Credit Union Administration;
1215             (iii) a person licensed as a principal broker under Title 61, Chapter 2f, Real Estate
1216                 Licensing and Practices Act, if the person is acting within the course and scope of

                                                       - 36 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                    S.B. 38


1217                 that license;
1218            (iv) a person licensed to practice law in this state if:
1219                 (A) the person renders the services described in Subsection [(3)(a)] (5)(a) within
1220                     the course and scope of the person's practice as an attorney; and
1221                 (B) the services described in Subsection [(3)(a)] (5)(a) are incidental to the
1222                     person's practice as an attorney;
1223            (v) a broker-dealer registered with the Securities and Exchange Commission or the
1224                 Commodity Futures Trading Commission if the broker-dealer is acting within the
1225                 course and scope of that regulation;
1226            (vi) a credit reporting agency if the services described in Subsection [(3)(a)] (5)(a) are
1227                 incidental to the credit reporting agency's services; or
1228            (vii) a person who provides debt-management services and is required to be
1229                 registered under Title 13, Chapter 42, Uniform Debt-Management Services Act.
1230    (6) "Division" means the Division of Consumer Protection established in Section 13-2-102.
1231    [(4)] (7) "Extension of credit" means the right to defer payment of debt or to incur debt and
1232        defer its payment, offered or granted primarily for personal, family, or household
1233        purposes.

1572    (3) "Charitable purpose" means:
1573        (a) [ ]any benevolent, educational, philanthropic, humane, patriotic, religious,
1574            eleemosynary, social welfare or advocacy, public health, environmental,
1575            conservation, civic, or other charitable objective; or
1576        (b) for the benefit of a public safety, law enforcement, or firefighter fraternal association.
1577    (4) "Charitable sales promotion" means an advertising or sales campaign, conducted by a
1578        commercial co-venturer, [which] that represents that the purchase or use of goods or
1579        services [offered by] that the commercial co-venturer offers will benefit, in whole or in
1580        part, a charitable organization or purpose.
1581    (5)(a) ["Charitable solicitation" or "solicitation"] "Charitable solicitation" means [any] a
1582        person's request, directly or indirectly, for money, credit, property, financial
1583        assistance, or [any other] another thing of value based on the plea or representation
1584        that [it will be used] the person will use the money, credit, property, financial
1585        assistance, or any other thing of value for a charitable purpose.
1586        (b) ["Charitable solicitation" or "solicitation" ] "Charitable solicitation" includes:
1587            (i) any of the following done, or purporting to be done, for a charitable purpose:
1588                (A) [any ] an oral or a written request, including [any] a request by telephone,
1589                     radio, television, or other advertising or communications media;
1590                (B) the distribution, circulation, or posting of [any] a handbill, written
1591                     advertisement, or publication; or
1592                (C) an application or other request for a [a ]private grant or, if made by an

                                                      - 47 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


1593                      individual, a public grant;[ or]
1594             (ii) the sale of, offer or attempt to sell, or request of donations in exchange for any
1595                 advertisement, membership, subscription, or other article in connection with
1596                 which [any] a person makes an appeal [is made for any] for a charitable purpose;
1597             (iii) [, or the ] a person's use of the name of [any] a charitable organization or
1598                 movement as an inducement or reason for making [any] a purchase or donation[,] ;
1599                 or[, ]
1600             (iv) in connection with [any] a sale or donation, a person stating or implying that the [
1601                 whole] person will give or donate all or [any] a part of the proceeds of [any] a sale
1602                 or donation [will go to or be donated ]to [any] a charitable purpose.
1603         (c) ["Charitable solicitation" or "solicitation" ] "Charitable solicitation" does not include
1604             an entity's application or other request for a public grant.
1605     (6) "Commercial co-venturer" means a person [who] that for profit [is ]regularly and
1606         primarily [engaged] engages in trade or commerce other than in connection with
1607         soliciting for a charitable organization or purpose.
1608     (7)(a) "Contribution" means the pledge or grant for a charitable purpose of any money or

1613             (iii) fees, dues, or assessments paid by members, when membership is conferred

1968          Section 39. Section 13-22-108, which is renumbered from Section 13-22-13 is renumbered

1970          [13-22-13] 13-22-108 (Effective 05/06/26). Prohibited practices.
1971       In connection with a charitable solicitation, each of the following acts and practices is
1972    prohibited:
1973    (1) stating or implying that registration constitutes endorsement or approval by the division
1974        or a governmental entity;
1975    (2) violating a requirement of this chapter or a rule made under this chapter;
1976    (3) making a false statement of a material fact or failing to state a material fact necessary to
1977        make statements made, in the context of the circumstances under which they are made,
1978        not misleading, whether in connection with a charitable solicitation or a filing with the
1979        division; and
1980    (4) violating an order issued by the division under Section [13-22-3] 13-22-102.

2129                            CHAPTER 23. Fitness Center Services Protection Act

2130                                            Part 1. General Provisions

2131          [13-23-2] 13-23-101 (Effective 05/06/26). Definitions.
2132       As used in this chapter:

2134        venture, corporation, limited liability company, or other entity used in carrying on a
2135        business.

       S.B. 38                                                                                      Enrolled Copy


2136     (2) "Consumer" means a purchaser of [health spa] fitness center services for consideration.

2157                     individual to develop a specific skill rather than develop or preserve physical
2158                     fitness, including gymnastics, tennis, rock climbing, or a winter sport;

2160                     individual to practice yoga or Pilates;
2161             (x) a private employer who owns and operates a facility exclusively for the benefit of

2165                         business; and
2166                     (B) is offered on a nonprofit basis;

2174                 business enterprise offers;

2839             Section 58. Section 13-26-103 is enacted to read:
2840             13-26-103 (Effective 05/06/26). Denial, suspension, or revocation of an
2841     application or registration.
2842         In accordance with Chapter 2, Division of Consumer Protection, and Title 63G, Chapter
2843     4, Administrative Procedures Act, the division may initiate adjudicative proceedings to deny,
2844     suspend, or revoke an application or registration if:
2845     (1) the division finds that the denial, suspension, or revocation is in the public interest; and

                                                        - 84 -

2846    (2)(a) the registration is incomplete, false, or misleading; or
2847        (b) the applicant or the applicant's principal:
2848            (i) violates, causes a violation, allows a violation, or fails to satisfy the requirements
2849                 of a provision of:
2850                 (A) this chapter; or
2851                 (B) a rule the division makes in accordance with this chapter;
2852            (ii) violates Chapter 11, Utah Consumer Sales Practices Act;
2853            (iii) is enjoined by a court, or is the subject of an administrative or judicial order
2854                 issued in Utah or another state, if the order:
2855                 (A) includes a finding or admission of fraud, breach of fiduciary duty, or material
2856                     misrepresentation; or
2857                 (B) is based on a finding of a lack of integrity, truthfulness, or mental competence;
2858            (iv) is convicted of a crime involving theft, fraud, or dishonesty;
2859            (v) obtains or attempts to obtain a registration by misrepresenting a material fact;
2860            (vi) fails to provide information the division requests;
2861            (vii) fails to pay an administrative fine the division or an administrative or judicial
2862                 order imposes; or
2863            (viii) fails to pay the fee to file a registration application or a renewal application.

3064          [13-26-11] 13-26-108 (Effective 05/06/26). Prohibited practices.
3065    (1) It is unlawful for a seller to:
3066        (a) solicit a prospective purchaser if the seller is not registered with the division or is
3067             exempt from registration under this chapter;
3068        (b) in connection with a telephone solicitation[ or a filing with the division], make or
3069             cause to be made a false [material ]statement or fail to disclose a material fact
3070             necessary to make the seller's statement not misleading;
3071        (c) make or authorize the making of a misrepresentation to a purchaser or prospective
3072             purchaser about the seller's compliance with this chapter;
3073        (d) fail to refund within 30 days any amount due a purchaser who exercises the right to
3074             cancel under Section [13-26-5] 13-26-105;
3075        (e) unless the seller is exempt under Section [13-26-4] 13-26-104, fail to orally advise a
3076             purchaser of the purchaser's right to cancel under Section [13-26-5] 13-26-105;
3077        (f) employ an inmate in a correctional facility for telephone soliciting operations when
3078             the employment would give the inmate access to an individual's personal data,
3079             including the individual's name, address, telephone number, Social Security number,
3080             credit card information, or physical description; or
3081        (g) cause or permit a solicitor to violate a provision of this chapter.
3082    (2) It is unlawful for a solicitor to:
3083        (a) use a fictitious personal name in connection with a telephone solicitation;

                                                       - 91 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                  Enrolled Copy


3084         (b) in connection with a telephone solicitation, make or cause to be made a false material
3085               statement or fail to disclose a material fact necessary to make the solicitor's statement

3087         (c) make a misrepresentation to a purchaser or prospective purchaser about the solicitor's
3088               compliance with this chapter; or
3089         (d) unless the solicitor is exempt under Section [13-26-4] 13-26-104, fail to orally advise
3090               a purchaser of the purchaser's right to cancel under Section [13-26-5] 13-26-105.
3091     (3) If a person knows or has reason to know that a seller or solicitor [is engaged] engages in
3092         an act or practice that violates this chapter, it is unlawful for the person to:
3093         (a) benefit from the seller's or solicitor's services; or
3094         (b) provide substantial assistance or support to the seller or solicitor.
3095     (4) A seller or a seller's solicitor may not:

3097         (b) omit from a filing with the division a material statement of fact required by:
3098               (i) this chapter; or
3099               (ii) a rule made by the division in accordance with this chapter; or
3100         (c) include in a filing with the division a material statement of fact that the seller or
3101               seller's principal knew or should have known to be false, deceptive, inaccurate, or
3102               misleading.

3104         regardless of whether the person receiving the solicitation agrees to the sale or to make a
3105         charitable donation.

3581              postsecondary school; or
3582         (b) two years after it is issued to an accredited postsecondary school.
3583     (9) A registration statement, and any certificate issued in accordance with this chapter, are
3584         not transferable.
3585     (10) Notwithstanding Subsection (8), the division may extend the period for which a
3586         registration certificate is effective so that expiration dates are staggered throughout the
3587         year.
3588     (11) To renew a registration certificate under this section, a postsecondary school shall
3589         submit a registration renewal application to the division at least 30 days before the day
3590         on which the postsecondary school's registration expires.

3652             Section 78. Section 13-41-202 is amended to read:
3653             13-41-202 (Effective 05/06/26). Enforcement -- Penalty.
3654     (1) The division shall enforce this chapter.
3655     (2) In determining whether to investigate, contact, or request information from a person in
3656         the enforcement of this chapter, the division shall consider:
3657         (a) whether a complaint, information, or evidence reasonably justifies further division
3658              inquiry;
3659         (b) the burden contact, investigation, or providing information places on the person;
3660         (c) the result of a previous investigation of the person, including whether the previous
3661              investigation suggests that the person did not violate this chapter;

                                                        - 108 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                     S.B. 38


3662        (d) whether the person may benefit from receiving information about requirements
3663            under this chapter; and
3664        (e) the potential gravity of harm to consumers, considering price, availability, and
3665            volume of a good or service.
3666    (3) In enforcing this chapter, the division may not publicly disclose the identity of a person
3667        the division investigates unless:
3668        (a) the person's identity is a matter of public record in an enforcement proceeding; or
3669        (b) the person consents to public disclosure.
3670    (4) In determining whether to impose penalties against a person who violates this chapter,
3671        the division shall consider:
3672        (a) the person's cost of doing business not accounted for in the total cost to the person
3673            for the good or service, including costs associated with a decrease in the supply
3674            available to a person who relies on a high volume of sales;
3675        (b) the person's efforts to comply with this chapter;
3676        (c) whether the average price charged by the person during the 30-day period
3677            immediately preceding the day on which the state of emergency is declared is
3678            artificially deflated because the good or service was on sale for a lower price than the
3679            person customarily charges for the good or service; and
3680        (d) any other factor that the division considers appropriate.
3681    (5)(a) If the division finds that a person has violated, or is violating, this chapter, the
3682        division may:
3683            (i) issue a cease and desist order; and
3684            (ii) subject to Subsection (5)(b), impose an administrative fine for each violation of
3685                 this chapter.
3686        (b) Each instance of charging an excessive price under Section 13-41-201 constitutes a
3687            separate violation, but in no case shall the administrative fine imposed under
3688            Subsection (5)(a) exceed double the excessive portion of the price the person charged.
3689    (6) The division may sue in a court of competent jurisdiction to enforce an order under
3690        Subsection (5).
3691    (7) In a suit brought under Subsection [(5)] (6), if the division prevails, the court may award
3692        the division:
3693        (a) court costs;
3694        (b) attorney fees; and
3695        (c) the division's costs incurred in the investigation of the violation of this chapter.

                                                       - 109 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


3696     (8) All money received through an administrative fine imposed, or judgment obtained,
3697         under this section shall be deposited in the Consumer Protection Education and Training
3698         Fund created by Section [13-2-8] 13-2-109.

4829        advice on an immigration matter including:

5096          Section 111. Section 13-49-306 is enacted to read:
5097          13-49-306 (Effective 05/06/26). Denial, suspension, or revocation of an
5098    application or registration.
5099       In accordance with Chapter 2, Division of Consumer Protection, and Title 63G, Chapter
5100    4, Administrative Procedures Act, the division may initiate adjudicative proceedings to deny,
5101    suspend, or revoke an application or registration if:
5102    (1) the division finds that the denial, suspension, or revocation is in the public interest; and
5103    (2)(a) the registration is incomplete, false, or misleading; or
5104        (b) the applicant:
5105            (i) violates, causes a violation, allows a violation, or fails to satisfy the requirements
5106                 of a provision of:
5107                 (A) this chapter; or
5108                 (B) a rule the division makes in accordance with this chapter;
5109            (ii) violates Chapter 11, Utah Consumer Sales Practices Act;
5110            (iii) is enjoined by a court, or is the subject of an administrative order or judicial
5111                 order issued in Utah or another state, if the order:
5112                 (A) includes a finding or admission of fraud, breach of fiduciary duty, or material
5113                     misrepresentation; or
5114                 (B) is based on a finding of lack of integrity, truthfulness, or mental competence;
5115            (iv) is convicted of a crime involving theft, fraud, or dishonesty;
5116            (v) obtains or attempts to obtain a registration by misrepresenting a material fact;
5117            (vi) fails to provide information the division requests;
5118            (vii) fails to pay an administrative fine that the division or an administrative or
5119                 judicial order imposes; or
5120            (viii) fails to pay the fee to file a registration application or a renewal application.
5121          Section 112. Section 13-49-401 is amended to read:
5122          13-49-401 (Effective 05/06/26). Unlawful acts.
5123    (1) It is unlawful for an immigration consultant to:

                                                      - 151 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                  Enrolled Copy


5124         (a) make a false or misleading statement to a client while providing [services] a service
5125              to [that] the client;
5126         (b) make a guarantee or promise to a client, unless the guarantee or promise is in writing
5127              and the immigration consultant has some basis in fact for making the guarantee or
5128              promise;
5129         (c) make a statement that the immigration consultant can or will obtain a special favor
5130              from or has special influence with the United States Citizenship and Immigration
5131              Services, or any other governmental agency, employee, or official, that may have a
5132              bearing on a client's immigration matter;[ or]
5133         (d) charge a client a fee for the referral of the client to another person for services that
5134              the immigration consultant cannot or will not provide to the client[.] ;
5135         (e) represent that the division or the state endorses the immigration consultant;
5136         (f) omit from a filing with the division a material statement of fact this chapter or a rule
5137              the division makes in accordance with this chapter requires; or
5138         (g) include in a filing with the division a material statement of fact that the immigration

5332             13-51-105 (Effective 05/06/26). Operating requirements.
5333     (1) A transportation network company shall maintain an agent for service of process in the
5334         state [and shall notify the division of the name and address of the agent] in accordance
5335         with Subsection 13-51-104(2).

5337         driver, a fare for a prearranged ride if the transportation network company:

5339              software application;
5340         (b) provides a passenger the rate [used] the transportation network company uses to
5341              calculate the fare for a prearranged ride; and
5342         (c) allows a passenger the option to obtain an estimated fare for a prearranged ride
5343              before the passenger enters a transportation network driver's vehicle.
5344     (3) For [each] a prearranged ride, a transportation network company shall:
5345         (a) before a passenger enters a transportation network driver's vehicle, display on the
5346              transportation network company's software application a picture of the transportation
5347              network driver;

5349              passenger that lists:
5350              (i) the prearranged ride's origin and destination;
5351              (ii) the prearranged ride's total time and distance; and
5352              (iii) an itemization of the total fare the passenger [paid] pays, if any; and
5353         (c) allow a passenger to notify a transportation network driver if a passenger has skis, a
5354              snowboard, other oversize luggage, or child restraint device.

5356         services:

5437          Section 121. Section 13-52-102 is amended to read:
5438                     CHAPTER 52. Residential Solar Energy Consumer Protection Act

5439          13-52-102 (Effective 05/06/26). Definitions.
5440       As used in this chapter:
5441    (1) "Actual energy production" means the average kilowatt-hours produced and measured
5442        by the residential solar energy system during the 12 consecutive months immediately
5443        following the residential solar energy system's activation.
5444    (2) "Customer" means a person [who] that, for primarily personal, family, or household
5445        purposes:
5446        (a) purchases, or is solicited to purchase, a residential solar energy system under a
5447            system purchase agreement;
5448        (b) leases, or is solicited to lease, a residential solar energy system under a system lease
5449            agreement; or
5450        (c) purchases, or is solicited to purchase, electricity under a power purchase agreement.
5451    (3) "Division" means the Division of Consumer Protection, established in [Section 13-2-1]
5452        Section 13-2-102.
5453    (4)(a) "Employee" means an individual whose compensation for federal income tax
5454        purposes is reported, or is required to be reported, on a W-2 form the employer issues.
5455        (b) "Employee" does not include an independent contractor whose manner and means of
5456            work performance are not subject to direction, supervision, or instruction from the
5457            person who [employed] employs the independent contractor.
5458    (5) "Estimated energy production" means the solar retailer's estimate, measured by
5459        kilowatt-hour, of how much energy the residential solar energy system will produce.
5460    (6) "Nameplate capacity" means the sum of the maximum rated outputs of all electrical
5461        generating equipment under specific conditions [designated by ]the manufacturer
5462        designates, as indicated on the nameplate physically attached to the equipment.
5463    (7) "Participant" means an owner, officer, director, member or manager of a limited liability
                                                     - 161 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                   Enrolled Copy


5464         company, principal, trustee, general or limited partner, sole proprietor, or an individual,
5465         with a controlling interest in an entity.
5466     (8) "Power purchase agreement" means an agreement:
5467         (a) between a customer and a solar retailer;
5468         (b) for the customer's purchase of electricity [generated by] that a residential solar energy
5469             system owned by the solar retailer generates; and
5470         (c) that provides for the customer to make payments over a term of at least five years.
5471     (9)(a) "Residential solar energy system" means a solar energy system that:
5472             (i) is installed in the state;
5473             (ii) generates electricity primarily for on-site consumption for personal, family, or
5474                  household purposes;
5475             (iii) is situated on no more than four units of residential real property; and
5476             (iv) has an electricity delivery capacity that exceeds one kilowatt.
5477         (b) "Residential solar energy system" does not include a generator that:
5478             (i) produces electricity; and
5479             (ii) is intended for occasional use.
5480     (10) "Sales representative" means an individual who:
5481         (a)(i) enters into a business relationship with a solar retailer to sell or attempt to sell a
5482             residential solar energy system through direct contact with customers and
5483             potential customers; and
5484             (ii) as part of the business relationship described in Subsection (10)(a), is
5485                  compensated, in whole or in part, by commission; or
5486         (b) sets appointments or discusses the benefits of solar energy with a potential customer
5487             on behalf of a solar retailer.
5488     (11) "Solar agreement" means a system purchase agreement, a system lease agreement, or a
5489         power purchase agreement.
5490     (12) "Solar energy system" means a system or configuration of solar energy devices that
5491         collects and uses solar energy to generate electricity.
5492     (13) "Solar retailer" means a person who:
5493         (a) sells or proposes to sell a residential solar energy system to a customer under a
5494             system purchase agreement;
5495         (b) owns the residential solar energy system that is the subject of a system lease
5496             agreement or proposed system lease agreement; or
5497         (c) sells or proposes to sell electricity to a customer under a power purchase agreement.

                                                         - 162 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                       S.B. 38


5498    (14) "System lease agreement" means an agreement:
5499        (a) under which a customer leases a residential solar energy system from a solar retailer;
5500            and
5501        (b) that provides for the customer to make payments over a term of at least five years for
5502            the lease of the residential solar energy system.
5503    (15) "System purchase agreement" means an agreement under which a customer purchases
5504        a residential solar energy system from a solar retailer.
5505          Section 122. Section 13-52-202 is amended to read:
5506          13-52-202 (Effective 05/06/26). Contents of disclosure statement for any solar
5507    agreement.
5508       If a solar retailer is proposing to enter any solar agreement with a potential customer, the
5509    disclosure statement required in Subsection 13-52-201(1) shall include:
5510    (1) a statement indicating that operations or maintenance services are not included as part

5516                  longer period not to exceed the anticipated 20-year useful life of the residential
5517                  solar energy system;

5519            source of those assumptions, including:

5522                  of the rate increase;

5524                  governmental or utility incentives;

5964                   administrator of the residential vocational or life skills program; and
5965             (ii) has failed to maintain [fiscal] financial responsibility.

5968         the information.

6093             Section 134. Section 13-57-202 is amended to read:
6094             13-57-202 (Effective 05/06/26). Maintenance funding provider operations --
6095     Prohibited acts.
6096     (1) A maintenance funding provider may only provide legal funding to an individual if the
6097         maintenance funding provider and the individual enter into a maintenance funding
6098         agreement that meets the requirements of Section 13-57-301.
6099     (2) Before executing a maintenance funding agreement, a maintenance funding provider
6100         shall file with the division a template of the maintenance funding agreement.
6101     (3) A maintenance funding provider may not:
6102         (a) pay or offer to pay a commission, referral fee, or any other form of consideration to
6103              the following for referring an individual to the maintenance funding provider:
6104              (i) an attorney authorized to practice law;
6105              (ii) a health care provider; or
6106              (iii) an employee, independent contractor, or other person affiliated with a person
6107                  described in Subsection (3)(a)(i) or (ii);
6108         (b) accept a commission, referral fee, or any other form of consideration from a person
6109              described in Subsection (3)(a) for referring an individual to the person;

                                                        - 180 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                      S.B. 38


6110        (c) refer an individual or potential individual to a person described in Subsection (3)(a),
6111            unless the referral is to a local or state bar association referral service;
6112        (d) intentionally advertise materially false or misleading information about the
6113            maintenance funding provider's services;
6114        (e) make or attempt to influence a decision relating to the conduct, settlement, or
6115            resolution of a legal action for which the maintenance funding provider provides
6116            legal funding;[ or]
6117        (f) knowingly pay or offer to pay court costs, filing fees, or attorney fees using legal
6118            funding[.] ;
6119        (g) represent that the division or the state endorses the maintenance funding provider;
6120        (h) omit from a filing with the division a material statement of fact that this chapter or a
6121            rule the division makes in accordance with this chapter requires; or
6122        (i) include in a filing with the division a material statement of fact that the maintenance
6123            funding provider or maintenance funding provider's principal knows or should know
6124            is false, deceptive, inaccurate, or misleading.
6125    (4) A maintenance funding provider shall provide an individual who enters a maintenance
6126        funding agreement a copy of the executed maintenance funding agreement.

6338            (i) a controller's disclosure of personal data to a processor who processes the personal
6339                data on behalf of the controller;
6340            (ii) a controller's disclosure of personal data to an affiliate of the controller;
6341            (iii) considering the context in which the consumer provided the personal data to the
6342                controller, a controller's disclosure of personal data to a third party if the purpose

6348                 providing a product or service requested by the consumer or a parent or legal
6349                 guardian of a child;

6352                      media; and
6353                 (B) does not restrict to a specific audience; or
6354             (vii) a controller's transfer of personal data to a third party as an asset that is part of a
6355                 proposed or actual merger, an acquisition, or a bankruptcy in which the third party
6356                 assumes control of all or part of the controller's assets.
6357     (32)(a) "Sensitive data" means:
6358             (i) personal data that reveals:
6359                 (A) an individual's racial or ethnic origin;
6360                 (B) an individual's religious beliefs;
6361                 (C) an individual's sexual orientation;
6362                 (D) an individual's citizenship or immigration status; or

6364                      health condition, or medical treatment or diagnosis by a health care
6365                      professional;
6366             (ii) the processing of genetic personal data or biometric data, if the processing is for
6367                 the purpose of identifying a specific individual; or
6368             (iii) specific geolocation data.
6369         (b) "Sensitive data" does not include personal data that reveals an individual's:
6370             (i) racial or ethnic origin, if the personal data are processed by a video
6371                 communication service; or
6372             (ii) if the personal data are processed by a person licensed to provide health care
6373                 under Title 26B, Chapter 2, Part 2, Health Care Facility Licensing and Inspection,
6374                 or Title 58, Occupations and Professions, information regarding an individual's
6375                 medical history, mental or physical health condition, or medical treatment or
6376                 diagnosis by a health care professional.
6377     (33)(a) "Specific geolocation data" means information derived from technology,
6378         including global position system level latitude and longitude coordinates, that directly
6379         identifies an individual's specific location, accurate within a radius of 1,750 feet or
6380         less.
6381         (b) "Specific geolocation data" does not include:

                                                       - 188 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                    S.B. 38


6382            (i) the content of a communication; or
6383            (ii) any data generated by or connected to advanced utility metering infrastructure
6384                systems or equipment for use by a utility.
6385    (34) "Synthetic data" means data that has been generated by computer algorithms or
6386        statistical models and does not contain personal data.
6387    (35)(a) "Targeted advertising" means displaying an advertisement to a consumer where
6388        the advertisement is selected based on personal data obtained from the consumer's
6389        activities over time and across nonaffiliated websites or online applications to predict
6390        the consumer's preferences or interests.
6391        (b) "Targeted advertising" does not include advertising:
6392            (i) based on a consumer's activities within a controller's website or online application
6393                or any affiliated website or online application;
6394            (ii) based on the context of a consumer's current search query or visit to a website or
6395                online application;
6396            (iii) directed to a consumer in response to the consumer's request for information,
6397                product, a service, or feedback; or
6398            (iv) processing personal data solely to measure or report advertising:
6399                (A) performance;
6400                (B) reach; or
6401                (C) frequency.
6402    (36) "Third party" means a person other than:
6403        (a) the consumer, controller, or processor; or
6404        (b) an affiliate or contractor of the controller or the processor.
6405    (37) "Trade secret" means information, including a formula, pattern, compilation, program,
6406        device, method, technique, or process, that:
6407        (a) derives independent economic value, actual or potential, from not being generally
6408            known to, and not being readily ascertainable by proper means by, other persons who
6409            can obtain economic value from the information's disclosure or use; and
6410        (b) is the subject of efforts that are reasonable under the circumstances to maintain the
6411            information's secrecy.

6455    powers -- Fees -- Rulemaking.
6456    (1) The division shall administer and enforce this chapter in accordance with Chapter 2,
6457        Division of Consumer Protection.
6458    (2) In addition to the division's enforcement powers under Chapter 2, Division of Consumer
6459        Protection:
6460        (a) the division director may impose an administrative fine of up to $2,500 for each act
6461            that is in violation of this chapter, including failure to insure or consider a vehicle
6462            value protection agreement as required under Subsection 13-64-202(1); and
6463        (b) the division may bring a civil action to enforce this chapter.
6464    (3) In a civil action by the division to enforce this chapter, the court may:
6465        (a) declare that an act or practice violates this chapter;
6466        (b) issue an injunction for a violation of this chapter;
6467        (c) order disgorgement of any money received after a violation of this chapter;
6468        (d) order payment of disgorged money to an injured individual;
6469        (e) impose a civil penalty of up to $2,500 for each violation of this chapter; or
6470        (f) award any other relief that the court deems reasonable and necessary.
6471    (4) If a court grants judgment or injunctive relief to the division, the court shall award the
6472        division:
6473        (a) reasonable attorney fees;
6474        (b) court costs; and
6475        (c) investigative fees.
6476    (5)(a) A person who violates an administrative or court order issued for a violation of
6477        this chapter is subject to a civil penalty of no more than $5,000 for each violation.
6478        (b) A civil penalty authorized under this section may be imposed in any civil action
6479            brought by the division.
6480        (c) The division shall deposit money received for the payment of a fine or civil penalty
6481            under this section into the Consumer Protection Education and Training Fund created

6781             13-68-401 (Effective 05/06/26). Unlawful acts.
6782     (1) It is unlawful for a lawyer referral consultant or [any other] another person to:
6783         (a) [make ] make, cause, or allow to be made a false or misleading statement to a client
6784              or prospective client while providing or soliciting services to that client or
6785              prospective client;
6786         (b) [make ] make, cause, or allow to be made a guarantee or promise to a client or
6787              prospective client, unless the guarantee or promise is in writing and there is basis in
6788              fact for making the guarantee or promise;
6789         (c) charge a client a fee for referral of the client to another person for services that the

                                                         - 200 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                       S.B. 38


6790            lawyer referral consultant cannot or will not provide to the client;[ or]
6791        (d) communicate with a prospective client for the purpose of obtaining or referring
6792            business if the communication concerns a disaster, or an action for personal injury or
6793            wrongful death, unless:
6794            (i) the disaster, injury, or death occurred more than 30 days before the
6795                communication;
6796            (ii) the prospective client is a person who has a prior familial, prior personal, or prior
6797                professional relationship with the lawyer to be referred, the lawyer referral
6798                consultant, or the person communicating with the prospective client;
6799            (iii) the prospective client initiates the communication[ is initiated by the prospective
6800                client]; or
6801            (iv) [the communication is requested by ]a third party who has a prior familial or
6802                prior close personal relationship with the prospective client[.] requests the
6803                communication;
6804        (e) represent that the division or the state endorses or approves the lawyer referral
6805            consultant;
6806        (f) omit from a filing with the division a material statement of fact that this chapter or a
6807            rule the division makes in accordance with this section requires; or
6808        (g) include in a filing with the division a material statement of fact that the lawyer
6809            referral consultant or the lawyer referral consultant's principal knows or should know
6810            is false, deceptive, inaccurate, or misleading.
6811    (2) A lawyer referral consultant may not translate a document or other information in a way
6812        that falsely represents or implies that the lawyer referral consultant is an attorney.
6813    [(2) A sign describing the prohibition described in Subsection (1)(c) shall be conspicuously
6814        displayed in the office of a lawyer referral consultant.]

7202          Section 163. Section 13-77-101 is amended to read:
7203          13-77-101 (Effective 05/06/26). Definitions.
7204       As used in this chapter:
7205    (1) "Artificial intelligence technology" means the same as that term is defined in Section
7206        13-72-101.
7207    (2) "Consumer transaction" means the same as that term is defined in Section 13-11-3.

7209        13-2-102.
7210    (4) "Generative artificial intelligence" means an artificial intelligence technology system
7211        that:
7212        (a) is trained on data;
7213        (b) is designed to simulate human conversation with a consumer through one or more of
7214            the following:
7215            (i) text;
7216            (ii) audio; or
7217            (iii) visual communication; and
7218        (c) generates non-scripted outputs similar to outputs created by a human, with limited or
7219            no human oversight.
7220    (5) "High-risk artificial intelligence interaction" means an interaction with generative
7221        artificial intelligence that involves:
7222        (a) the collection of sensitive personal information, including:

7224            (ii) financial data; or
7225            (iii) biometric data;
7226        (b) the provision of personalized recommendations, advice, or information that could
7227            reasonably be relied upon to make significant personal decisions, including the

7344          Section 167. Section 13-78-103 is amended to read:
7345          13-78-103 (Effective 05/06/26). Regulation of earned wage access services.
7346    (1) A provider is exempt from Title 7, Financial Institutions Act, and Title 12, Collection
7347        Agencies.
7348    (2) A provider shall:
7349        (a) develop and implement procedures to address consumer questions and complaints;
7350        (b) before entering into an agreement for earned wage access services with a consumer,
7351            clearly and conspicuously disclose:
7352            (i) the consumer's rights under the agreement;
7353            (ii) all fees; and
7354            (iii) any voluntary tip, gratuity, or donation opportunities;
7355        (c)(i) obtain customer consent to changes to terms and conditions of the earned wage
7356            access services; or
7357            (ii) clearly and conspicuously disclose any material changes to terms and conditions
7358                of the earned wage access services at least 30 days before the effective date of the
7359                material changes;
7360        (d) allow a consumer to cancel the use of earned wage access services at any time
7361            without incurring a penalty;
7362        (e) offer at least one no-fee option to receive funds and clearly and conspicuously
7363            disclose how to select the no-fee option to receive funds;
7364        (f) comply with all applicable local, state, and federal privacy and information security
7365            laws;
7366        (g) deliver funds through any method agreed upon by the consumer and provider;
7367        (h) reimburse overdraft or non-sufficient fund fees the consumer incurs as a result of the

                                                      - 217 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


7368             provider's error in disclosed or actual payment amount or payment date unless the
7369             funds were acquired by a consumer through fraudulent means;
7370         (i) clearly and conspicuously disclose the voluntary nature of tips, gratuities, or
7371             donations and ensure the availability or terms of the earned wage access services are
7372             not contingent upon the payment of tips, gratuities, or donations;
7373         (j) provide information to a consumer on how to file a complaint with the division;
7374         (k) provide the following disclosures to a consumer at the time the consumer makes a
7375             request for funds:
7376             (i) the anticipated timeline the consumer will receive the requested funds;
7377             (ii) the amount of funds the consumer has requested;
7378             (iii) the amount of the fee charged;
7379             (iv) the amount of funds the consumer will receive;
7380             (v) the account that will receive the funds; and
7381             (vi) the date the provider is authorized to withdraw funds from the consumer's
7382                 account, including fees and voluntary payments; and
7383         (l) before initiating an advance, require the customer to acknowledge receiving the
7384             opportunity to view all disclosures listed in Subsection (2)(k) and any costs and fees.
7385     (3) A provider may not:
7386         (a) compel a consumer to repay funds by:
7387             (i) using or threatening to use civil lawsuits, outbound calls, third-party collections,
7388                 or debt sales;
7389             (ii) reporting or threatening to report nonpayment to consumer reporting agencies; or
7390             (iii) charging or threatening to charge interest, finance charges, late fees, or other
7391                 penalties for nonpayment;
7392         (b) use a consumer's credit report or credit score as defined by 15 U.S.C. Sec. 1681 et
7393             seq., to determine eligibility for earned wage access services;
7394         (c) accept payment with a credit card or charge card;
7395         (d) condition receipt of funds based on fees, tips, gratuities, or donations;
7396         (e) mislead a consumer about the voluntary nature of tips, gratuities, or donations;
7397         (f) charge a consumer a fee, interest, or any other penalty for failure to repay outstanding
7398             proceeds, fees, tips, or gratuities;[ or]
7399         (g) provide earned wage access services in this state without first registering with the
7400             division[.] ;
7401         (h) represent that the division or the state endorses the provider;

                                                        - 218 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                       S.B. 38


7402        (i) omit from a filing with the division a material statement of fact that this chapter or a
7403            rule the division makes in accordance with this chapter requires; or
7404        (j) include in a filing with the division a material statement of fact that the provider or
7405            the provider's principal knows or should know is false, deceptive, inaccurate, or
7406            misleading.
7407    (4) Each act performed in violation of Subsection (3) is a separate violation of this chapter.
7408    (5) The provider may prohibit a consumer from requesting a transaction if the consumer has
7409        outstanding unpaid proceeds from a previous transaction with the provider.
7410    (6) A provider is not entitled to the exemption described by Subsection 13-11-22(1)(d).

8072         (m) for one or more guests of an officeholder or candidate to attend an event, meeting,
8073             or conference described in this Subsection (2), including related travel expenses and
8074             other expenses, if attendance by the guest is for a primary purpose described in
8075             Subsection (2)(g)(iv) or (vi); or
8076         (n) to pay childcare expenses of:
8077             (i) a candidate while the candidate is engaging in campaign activity; or
8078             (ii) an officeholder while the officeholder is engaging in the duties of an officeholder.
8079     (3)(a) The lieutenant governor shall enforce this chapter prohibiting a personal use
8080         expenditure by:
8081             (i) evaluating a financial statement to identify a personal use expenditure; and

                                                       - 238 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                     S.B. 38


8082            (ii) commencing an informal adjudicative proceeding in accordance with Title 63G,
8083                  Chapter 4, Administrative Procedures Act, if the lieutenant governor has probable
8084                  cause to believe a candidate or officeholder has made a personal use expenditure.
8085        (b) Following the proceeding, the lieutenant governor may issue a signed order requiring
8086            a candidate or officeholder who has made a personal use expenditure to:
8087            (i) remit an administrative penalty of an amount equal to 50% of the personal use
8088                  expenditure to the lieutenant governor; and
8089            (ii) deposit the amount of the personal use expenditure in the campaign account from
8090                  which the personal use expenditure was disbursed.
8091        (c) The lieutenant governor shall deposit money received under Subsection (3)(b)(i) [in]
8092            into the General Fund.
