8427          Section 175. Section 51-9-801 is amended to read:
8428          51-9-801 (Effective 05/06/26). Opioid Litigation Proceeds Fund.
8429    (1) As used in this section:
8430        (a) "Fund" means the Opioid Litigation Proceeds Fund created in this section.
8431        (b)(i) "Principal" means money deposited into the fund in accordance with this
8432            section.
8433            (ii) "Principal" does not include:
8434                 (A) earnings credited to the fund, including interest and dividends; or
8435                 (B) up to $21 million of money described in Subsection (4) that the Legislature
8436                       allocated through July 1, 2026, to be spent for various purposes through June
8437                       30, 2027.
8438    (2) There is created the Opioid Litigation Proceeds Fund.
8439    (3) The fund consists of:
8440        (a) any money deposited into the fund in accordance with Subsection (4);
8441        (b) interest and dividends earned on money in the fund; and
8442        (c) money appropriated to the fund by the Legislature.
8443    (4) Notwithstanding Sections [13-2-8] 13-2-109 and 67-5-40, after reimbursement to the
8444        attorney general and the Department of Commerce for expenses related to the matters
8445        described in Subsection (4)(a) or (b), the following shall be deposited into the fund:
8446        (a) all money received by the attorney general or the Department of Commerce as a
8447            result of any judgment, settlement, or compromise of claims pertaining to alleged
8448            violations of law related to the manufacture, marketing, distribution, or sale of
8449            opioids from a case designated as an opioid case by the attorney general in a legal
8450            services contract; and
8451        (b) all money received by the attorney general or the Department of Commerce as a
8452            result of any multistate judgment, settlement, or compromise of claims pertaining to
8453            alleged violations of law related to the manufacture, marketing, distribution, or sale
8454            of opioids.
8455    (5) The state treasurer shall:

                                                      - 249 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


8456         (a) invest the money in the fund:
8457              (i) for the benefit of the people of the state in perpetuity; and
8458              (ii) with the following goals, in order of priority:
8459                  (A) providing for growth of the principal; and
8460                  (B) fund stability; and
8461         (b) invest and manage fund money as a prudent investor would by:
8462              (i) considering the purpose, terms, distribution requirements, and other circumstances
8463                  of the fund; and
8464              (ii) exercising reasonable care, skill, and caution in order to meet the standard of care
8465                  of a prudent investor.
8466     (6) The state treasurer may deduct any administrative costs incurred by managing the fund
8467         from earnings generated by investments in the fund.
8468     (7)(a) The Legislature may not appropriate principal from the fund.
8469         (b) Subject to appropriation by the Legislature, money in the account other than
8470              principal shall be used:
8471              (i) to address the effects of alleged violations of law related to the manufacture,
8472                  marketing, distribution, or sale of opioids; or
8473              (ii) if applicable, in accordance with the terms of a settlement agreement described in
8474                  Subsection (4)(a) or (b) entered into by the state.

8491            lines.
8492        (d) "Subscriber" means a corporation, person, or government, or a person acting legally
8493            on behalf of a corporation, person, or government who has purchased public
8494            telecommunications services from a telecommunications corporation.
8495    (2) No telecommunications corporation or [its] the telecommunications corporation's agents
8496        shall make any change or authorize a different telecommunications corporation to make
8497        any change in the provider of any public telecommunications service to a subscriber
8498        unless it complies, at a minimum, with Subsections (2)(a) through (e).[ ] This Subsection
8499        (2) does not apply to a telecommunications corporation that effectuates a change in
8500        service provider [pursuant to] in accordance with a change authorization submitted or
8501        requested by another telecommunications corporation.
8502        (a) The telecommunications corporation or [its] the telecommunications corporation's
8503            agents shall, at a minimum, inform the subscriber of the nature, extent, and rates of
8504            the service being offered and any charges associated with the change.
8505        (b) Notwithstanding Section [13-26-4] 13-26-104, changes in provider of
8506            telecommunication service accomplished through telephone solicitation shall comply
8507            with the Telephone Fraud Prevention Act, Sections [13-26-2] 13-26-101, [13-26-8]
8508            13-26-106, [13-26-10] 13-26-107, and [13-26-11] 13-26-108.
8509        (c) For sales of residential service or small commercial subscriber service, the
8510            telecommunications corporation or [its] the telecommunications corporation's agents
8511            shall confirm that the subscriber is aware of any charges that the subscriber must pay
8512            associated with the change and that the subscriber authorizes the change of provider.
8513            The subscriber's authorization to change the provider shall be confirmed by any one
8514            of the following methods:
8515            (i) obtaining the subscriber's written authorization;
8516            (ii) having the subscriber's oral authorization verified by an independent third party;
8517                or
8518            (iii) any means provided by rule of the Federal Communications Commission or the
8519                commission.
8520        (d) If the subscriber is not an individual, an authorization shall be valid only if given by
8521            an authorized representative of the subscriber.
8522        (e)(i) The written authorization to change the provider shall be signed by the
8523            subscriber and shall contain a clear, conspicuous, and unequivocal request by the

                                                     - 251 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                  Enrolled Copy


8524             subscriber for a change of telecommunications provider.
8525             (ii) A written authorization is not valid if it is presented to the subscriber for signature
8526                 in connection with a sweepstakes, game of chance, or any other means prohibited
8527                 by commission rule.
8528             (iii) Nothing in this section shall be construed to prohibit any person from offering a
8529                 premium, incentive, or a thing of value to another as consideration for authorizing
8530                 a change of telecommunications service provider, provided that no element of
8531                 chance or skill is associated with the offer of the premium, incentive, or thing of
8532                 value or its receipt.
8533     (3) The confirmation by a third-party verifier shall, at a minimum:
8534         (a) confirm the subscriber's identity with information unique to the customer, unless the
8535             customer refuses to provide identifying information, then that fact shall be noted;
8536         (b) confirm that the subscriber agrees to the requested change in telecommunications
8537             service providers; and
8538         (c) confirm that the subscriber has the authority to select the provider as the provider of
8539             that service.
8540     (4) A third-party verifier shall meet each of the following criteria:
8541         (a) any criteria for third-party verifiers set by the Federal Communications Commission;
8542         (b) not be directly or indirectly managed, controlled, directed, or owned wholly or in
8543             part:
8544             (i) by the telecommunications corporation or [its] the telecommunications
8545                 corporation's agents that seek to provide the telecommunications service or by any
8546                 corporation, firm, or person who directly or indirectly manages, controls, directs,
8547                 or owns more than 5% of the telecommunications corporation; or
8548             (ii) by the marketing entity that seeks to market the telecommunications service or by
8549                 any corporation, firm, or person who directly or indirectly manages, controls,
8550                 directs, or owns more than 5% of the marketing entity;
8551         (c) operate from facilities physically separated from:
8552             (i) those of the telecommunications corporation or its agents that seek to provide the
8553                 subscriber's telecommunications service; or
8554             (ii) those of the marketing entity that seeks to market a telecommunications service to
8555                 the subscriber; and
8556         (d) not derive commissions or compensation based upon the number of change
8557             authorizations verified.

                                                       - 252 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                      S.B. 38


8558    (5) A telecommunications corporation or its agents seeking to verify the change
8559        authorization shall connect the subscriber to the third-party verifier or arrange for the
8560        third-party verifier to call the subscriber to verify the change authorization.
8561    (6) A third-party verifier that obtains the subscriber's oral verification regarding the change
8562        shall record that verification by obtaining appropriate verification data.
8563    (7)(a) The record verifying a subscriber's change of provider shall be available to the
8564        subscriber upon request.
8565        (b) Information obtained from the subscriber through verification may not be used for
8566            any other purpose.
8567        (c) Any intentional unauthorized release of the information in Subsection (7)(b) is
8568            grounds for penalties or other action by the commission or remedies provided by law
8569            to the aggrieved subscriber against the telecommunications corporation, third-party
8570            verifier, their agents, or their employees who are responsible for the violation.
8571    (8) The third-party verification shall occur in the same language as that in which the change
8572        was solicited.
8573    (9) The verification requirements described in this section shall apply to all changes in the
8574        provider of any public telecommunications service.
8575    (10) The commission may promulgate rules:
8576        (a) necessary to implement this section;
8577        (b) consistent with any rules promulgated by the Federal Communications Commission;
8578            and
8579        (c) in a nondiscriminatory and competitively neutral manner.
8580    (11)(a) Each subscriber may elect to require the telecommunications corporation
8581        providing the subscriber's local exchange service to implement a freeze until the
8582        subscriber provides authorization for a change to another provider of public
8583        telecommunications services.
8584        (b) Once a subscriber has elected the freeze option under Subsection (11)(a), the
8585            telecommunications corporation providing the subscriber's local exchange service
8586            may not process a request to change the subscriber to another provider of
8587            telecommunications services without prior authorization directly from the subscriber.
8588    (12)(a) Whenever the subscriber's provider of a telecommunications service changes, the
8589        new provider shall:
8590            (i) retain a record of the verified change authorization consistent with requirements of
8591                  the Federal Communications Commission or rules issued by the commission; and

                                                      - 253 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                  Enrolled Copy


8592              (ii) be responsible for providing a conspicuous notice of the change within 30 days of
8593                  the effective date of the change of service.
8594         (b) At a minimum, the notice in Subsection (12)(a)(ii) shall identify the new provider,
8595              contain a general description of the service and price, and provide information
8596              necessary for the subscriber to have questions answered or to rescind the change.
8597     (13) Any bill shall identify each telecommunications service provider of telecommunication
8598         service for which billing is rendered.
8599     (14)(a) Any person or provider of telecommunications service inadvertently or
8600         knowingly designating or changing the subscriber's telecommunications service
8601         provider in violation of this section shall refund to the subscriber any amounts
8602         required by the rules of the Federal Communications Commission and the
8603         commission.
8604         (b) The unauthorized provider in Subsection (14)(a) additionally shall:
8605              (i) bear all costs of restoring the customer to the service of the subscriber's original
8606                  service provider; and
8607              (ii) pay to any other telecommunications provider any fees set by the commission for
8608                  the designation or change.
8609     (15) Proceedings for violations of this section may be commenced by request for agency
8610         action filed with the commission by a subscriber, a telecommunications corporation, the
8611         Division of Public Utilities, or by the commission on its own motion.
8612     (16) Any telecommunications corporation, its agents, or a third-party verifier who violates
8613         this section or rules adopted to implement this section shall be subject to the provisions
8614         of Sections 54-7-23 through 54-7-29.
8615     (17) The commission is granted authority to enforce provisions relating to an unauthorized
8616         telecommunication service provider change in interstate and intrastate
8617         telecommunication service involving telecommunications corporations operating in the
8618         state.

8672        The following records are protected if properly classified by a governmental entity:

8674         provided the governmental entity with the information specified in Section 63G-2-309;

8679               ability of the governmental entity to obtain necessary information in the future;
8680         (b) the person submitting the information has a greater interest in prohibiting access than
8681               the public in obtaining access; and

8683               information specified in Section 63G-2-309;

9255        of the content.

9257            a content provider under Subsection (2).

9268        (a) properly rating content; or

9278             Section 182. Section 76-5c-402 is amended to read:
9279             76-5c-402 (Effective 05/06/26). Data service providers -- Internet content
9280     harmful to minors.
9281     (1)(a) Upon request by a consumer, a service provider shall filter content to prevent the
9282         transmission of material harmful to minors to the consumer.
9283         (b) A service provider complies with Subsection (1)(a) if the service provider makes a
9284              good faith effort to apply a generally accepted and commercially reasonable method
9285              of filtering.
9286         (c) At the time of a consumer's subscription to a service provider's service, the service
9287              provider shall notify the consumer in a conspicuous manner that the consumer may
9288              request to have material harmful to minors blocked under Subsection (1)(a).
9289     (2) [The Division of Consumer Protection within the Department of Commerce ] A service
9290         provider shall[:] ,
9291         [(a) every other year request from each service provider information on] no later than
9292              July 1 of each even-numbered year, provide information to the division that describes
9293              how the service provider complies with Subsection (1)(a)[;] .
9294     [(b)] (3) The division shall:
9295         (a) publish on the division's website a compilation of the information the division
9296              receives under Subsection [(2)(a)] (2); and

9298              odd-numbered year.
9299              [(c) update the compilation described in Subsection (2)(b) every other year.]
9300     [(3)] (4)(a) A service provider may comply with Subsection (1)(a) by providing

9302         that the filtering does not affect or interfere with access to [Internet] internet content
9303         for consumers who do not request filtering under Subsection (1)(a).

9306            reasonable method of filtering to block the receipt of material harmful to minors.

9319        (b) A service provider that intentionally or knowingly violates Subsection (1)(c) is
9320            subject to a civil fine up to $10,000.
9321    [(6)] (7) [A proceeding] Only the attorney general may bring an action to impose a civil fine
9322        under Subsection [(5) may only be brought by the attorney general] (6).
9323          Section 183. Section 78B-3-112 is amended to read:
9324          78B-3-112 (Effective 05/06/26). Action for mistaken or fraudulent transaction on
9325    a reversible blockchain.
9326    (1) As used in this section:
9327        (a) "Blockchain" means a digital ledger of transactions:
9328            (i) that is distributed across multiple nodes;
9329            (ii) that is mathematically verified; and
9330            (iii) where the validity of transactions is maintained by consensus of nodes.
9331        (b) "Blockchain administrator" means a person that is responsible for maintaining and
9332            overseeing a blockchain.
9333        (c) "Division" means the Division of Consumer Protection created in Section [13-2-1]
9334            13-2-102.
9335        (d) "Fraudulent transaction" means a transaction that a person undertakes with the intent
9336            to deceive another person, including a transaction that involves:
9337            (i) false representation;
9338            (ii) omissions of material fact; or
9339            (iii) the use of a false or stolen identity.

                                                       - 275 -
       S.B. 38                                                                                 Enrolled Copy


9340         (e) "Node" means a computer connected to a blockchain.
9341         (f) "Proof of identity" means government-issued identification that contains the
9342             following information:
9343             (i) a person's name;
9344             (ii) an individual's date of birth;
9345             (iii) a person's address, which is:
9346                 (A) for an individual, a residential or business street address;
9347                 (B) for an individual who does not have a residential or business street address, a
9348                      Post Office box number or the residential or business street address of next of
9349                      kin or of another contact individual; or
9350                 (C) for a person other than an individual, the principal place of business; and
9351             (iv) an identification number, which is:
9352                 (A) for a United States person, a taxpayer identification number; or
9353                 (B) for a non-United States person, a taxpayer identification number, passport
9354                      number and country of issuance, alien identification card number, or the
9355                      number and country of issuance of any other government-issued document
9356                      evidencing nationality or residence and bearing a photograph or similar
9357                      safeguard.
9358         (g) "Reversible blockchain" means a blockchain that:
9359             (i) requires the blockchain's users to:
9360                 (A) provide proof of identity to the blockchain administrator;
9361                 (B) acknowledge and agree that all transactions occurring on the blockchain are
9362                      subject to reversal by a sheriff node; and
9363                 (C) agree to be subject to jurisdiction of a court in Utah; and
9364             (ii) requires the blockchain administrator to:
9365                 (A) verify a user's identity by checking the user's proof of identity against
9366                      government-issued identification databases; and
9367                 (B) maintain records of a user's proof of identity for a minimum of five years.
9368         (h) "Sheriff node" means the same as that term is defined in Section 67-5-39.
9369         (i) "Transaction" means the transfer of digital assets, rights, privileges, or obligations
9370             from one person to another that occurs on a blockchain.
9371         (j)(i) "User" means a person that interacts with a blockchain.
9372             (ii) "User" includes a person that is:
9373                 (A) sending or receiving transactions;

                                                       - 276 -
       Enrolled Copy                                                                                     S.B. 38


9374                (B) accessing data stored on the blockchain;
9375                (C) participating in consensus or governance mechanisms;
9376                (D) running a node on the blockchain;
9377                (E) interacting with smart contracts or decentralized applications; or
9378                (F) holding or managing digital assets.
9379    (2) A plaintiff may bring a cause of action against a person to reverse:
9380        (a) a fraudulent transaction if:
9381            (i) the transaction occurred on a reversible blockchain;
9382            (ii) the plaintiff entered into the transaction with reasonable reliance on the person's:
9383                (A) fraudulent representation;
9384                (B) omission of material fact; or
9385                (C) use of a false or stolen identity; and
9386            (iii) the plaintiff was injured as a result of that reasonable reliance; or
9387        (b) a mistaken transaction if:
9388            (i) the transaction occurs on a reversible blockchain;
9389            (ii) the transaction resulted in a transfer of assets:
9390                (A) to the wrong recipient; or
9391                (B) in the wrong amount; and
9392            (iii) the recipient's refusal to return the assets resulted in the unjust enrichment of the
9393                recipient.
9394    (3) Upon a finding of a mistaken or fraudulent transaction, the court shall issue an order to
9395        the Office of the Attorney General to reverse the transaction in accordance with Section
9396        67-5-39.
